**Q:** what’s the pt for micron

**A:**

- Micron has undergone a structural rerating, moving from a cyclical commodity business to a critical AI infrastructure provider, with its HBM supply effectively sold out through 2026.

- Analyst sentiment is aggressively bullish following the company's recent trillion-dollar market cap milestone; UBS currently holds a street-high price target of **$1,625.00**, implying significant upside from current levels near **$915.00**.

- While the consensus price target sits lower at approximately **$607.72** due to the inclusion of older ratings, the most recent analyst revisions from firms like Melius Research and UBS have clustered in the **$700.00** to **$1,625.00** range.

- The primary catalyst remains the persistent supply bottleneck in high-bandwidth memory (HBM), which is forcing hyperscalers into long-term, fixed-price contracts that decouple Micron's earnings from traditional memory boom-bust cycles.

## 2026-05-28 00:34:56 UTC

**Asker:** Tony Astro (`mikathelegend`) in #💬-stonks-yapping-💬

**Q:** What’s the 2x lev etf for pltr that gets good amount of volume per day

**A:**

- **PLTU** (Direxion Daily PLTR Bull 2X ETF) is generally the most liquid, frequently seeing daily volume in the millions of shares.

- **PTIR** (GraniteShares 2x Long PLTR Daily ETF) is the other primary contender with substantial assets under management and high liquidity, making it a viable alternative if you're looking for tight spreads.

- **PLTG** (Leverage Shares 2x Long PLTR Daily ETF) exists, but its volume is significantly lower—often in the low hundreds of thousands—so you'll likely face wider spreads and more slippage compared to the Direxion or GraniteShares products.

Stick to PLTU or PTIR if you're actually trying to execute size without getting eaten by the spread.

"3. WHAT TO WATCH | ### Today

No major scheduled catalysts left in today's session. Watch for US-Iran headlines (an MOU is possible inside days, per JPMorgan) and for any oil futures reaction if Hormuz shipping status changes.

### This Week

- **Tomorrow (May 28, 8:30 AM ET): Core PCE Price Index (MoM) + GDP Growth 2nd Estimate.** Core PCE estimate is +0.3% MoM matching prior, GDP 2nd Est is 2.0% (up from 0.5% prior). A hot PCE reads through to the hawkish-Fed thread and extends the real-yield grind, a benign print is the catalyst the squeeze-risk setup has been waiting for. GDP at 2.0% would confirm the inelasticity thesis (capex masking consumer weakness).
- **Monday (June 1, 10:00 AM ET): ISM Manufacturing PMI.** Prior 52.7. Confirmation of expansion would extend the AI-capex bid into industrial names, sub-50 would be the first survey-data crack the bull case has not had to defend.
- **Wednesday (June 3, 10:00 AM ET): ISM Services PMI.** Prior 53.6. Same shape as Manufacturing but more leveraged to consumer trends, the read-through to the $WMT and discretionary theme is direct.
- **Near term: US-Iran MOU.** JPMorgan suggests a Memorandum of Understanding (a framework deal short of a full ceasefire) is possible within days. A clean ceasefire announcement is the binary that resolves the oil setup and triggers the rotation the Hormuz theme above describes.
- **Near term: Kevin Warsh Fed Chair tenure.** ING, TS Lombard, and one other desk flag Warsh's path to the chair as a topic without staking a directional view yet. Watch for headlines on confirmation timing, which would set the rate-path debate for the back half of the year."

[f.jamal's message to you]
What u think

**A:**

The market is currently running on a "capex-blind" thesis where AI infrastructure spending is masking significant consumer weakness, and that's a dangerous game to play. If the ISM prints or PCE data show the first real crack in that capex floor, the unwind will be violent because the market is priced for perfection, not for a reality where the consumer is actually tapped out. 

The US-Iran MOU is a binary headline that’s already being priced as a "done deal" by the oil markets, so any denial or delay is going to trigger a sharp reversal in energy and risk assets. Warsh is the ultimate wild card; the market liked the initial nomination, but his actual policy path is a massive unknown that will eventually collide with the current AI-fueled liquidity environment.

**A:**

Gamma Exposure (**GEX**) measures the aggregate gamma position of market makers (MMs) relative to a specific strike price. It’s the primary mechanism for understanding how MMs are forced to hedge their books as the market moves.

When **GEX is positive**, MMs are net long gamma. To remain delta-neutral, they must sell into strength and buy into weakness, which acts as a volatility dampener and creates "gravity" around the current price.

When **GEX is negative**, MMs are net short gamma. They are forced to buy into strength and sell into weakness to hedge, which creates a positive feedback loop that amplifies volatility and leads to rapid, directional moves.

The **"Gamma Flip" (Zero GEX)** is the critical pivot point where MMs transition from long to short gamma. Price action often stalls or reverses here because the hedging requirement shifts from dampening to amplifying, making it a key level for institutional positioning.

For resources, **SpotGamma** is the industry standard for retail-accessible GEX data and analytics. Beyond paid tools, focus on understanding the **open interest (OI)** distribution across the option chain; you can calculate a rough GEX approximation by looking at the concentration of call vs. put OI at key strikes to identify where the "flip" likely sits"flip" likely resides.

"Gamma Exposure (**GEX**) measures the aggregate gamma position of market makers (MMs) relative to a specific strike price. It’s the primary mechanism for understanding how MMs are forced to hedge their books as the market moves.

When **GEX is positive**, MMs are net long gamma. To remain delta-neutral, they must sell into strength and buy into weakness, which acts as a volatility dampener and creates "gravity" around the current price.

When **GEX is negative**, MMs are net short gamma. They are forced to buy into strength and sell into weakness to hedge, which creates a positive feedback loop that amplifies volatility and leads to rapid, directional moves.

The **"Gamma Flip" (Zero GEX)** is the critical pivot point where MMs transition from long to short gamma. Price action often stalls or reverses here because the hedging requirement shifts from dampening to amplifying, making it a key level for institutional positioning.

For resources, **SpotGamma** is the industry standard for retail-accessible GEX data and analytics. Beyond paid tools, focus on understanding the **open interest (OI)** distribution across the option chain; you can calculate a rough GEX approximation by looking at the concentration of call vs. put OI at key strikes to identify where the "flip" likely sits"flip" likely resides."

[YOU said earlier]: Gamma Exposure (**GEX**) measures the aggregate gamma position of market makers (MMs) relative to a specific strike price. It’s the primary mechanism for understanding how MMs are forced to hedge their books as the market moves.

"Gamma Exposure (**GEX**) measures the aggregate gamma position of market makers (MMs) relative to a specific strike price. It’s the primary mechanism for understanding how MMs are forced to hedge their books as the market moves.

When **GEX is positive**, MMs are net long gamma. To remain delta-neutral, they must sell into strength and buy into weakness, which acts as a volatility dampener and creates "gravity" around the current price.

When **GEX is negative**, MMs are net short gamma. They are forced to buy into strength and sell into weakness to hedge, which creates a positive feedback loop that amplifies volatility and leads to rapid, directional moves.

The **"Gamma Flip" (Zero GEX)** is the critical pivot point where MMs transition from long to short gamma. Price action often stalls or reverses here because the hedging requirement shifts from dampening to amplifying, making it a key level for institutional positioning.

For resources, **SpotGamma** is the industry standard for retail-accessible GEX data and analytics. Beyond paid tools, focus on understanding the **open interest (OI)** distribution across the option chain; you can calculate a rough GEX approximation by looking at the concentration of call vs. put OI at key strikes to identify where the "flip" likely sits"flip" likely resides."

[YOU said earlier]: Gamma Exposure (**GEX**) measures the aggregate gamma position of market makers (MMs) relative to a specific strike price. It’s the primary mechanism for understanding how MMs are forced to hedge their books as the market moves.

When **GEX is positive**, MMs are net long gamma. To remain delta-neutral, they must sell into strength and buy into weakness, which acts as a volatility dampener and creates "gravity" around the current price.

When **GEX is negative**, MMs are net short gamma. They are forced to buy into strength and sell into weakness to hedge, which creates a positive feedback loo

He doesn't post much about his own PnL, preferring to spend his time debugging why the bot can't track Kyle's trades or complaining about API limitations. He is the guy you go to when the bot breaks, and he is usually deep in the backend logic, occasionally dropping into #stonks-yapping to remind everyone that he’s the one pulling the strings behind the curtain. He trades perps on the side and views the market through a highly technical, logic-driven lens.

**Recent trades.**
- "I be trading perps lately" — the only admission of active trading, with zero receipts provided.
- Successfully deployed the back-end database logic for the tracker, but the "win/loss" calculation still treats expired options as total losses regardless of actual exit price. A functional win for the code, a disaster for the accuracy.

**Recent personal life.**

- Deeply obsessed with tax-advantaged accounts, specifically the $72k max contribution strategy; he's the guy at the party lecturing you about your marginal tax rate.

While he talks about trading crypto perps, he spends almost 100% of his screen time obsessed with other people's PnL, treating the chat like a live-action database. He is the guy you ping when the bot breaks, and he is seemingly more interested in the logic of the trade than the trade itself. High technical utility, low trading transparency.

**Voice.**
- "Bot seems down for some reason lemme check" — standard maintenance alert
- "Actually it's not even working so it's going down today" — closing out a bad code project
- "Post like Abe, it doesn't know wtf u doing" — when a user fails to format their trades for the bot to parse
- "I might need to implement some way for you to correct it" — when his bot hallucinated a loss
- "Max that shit man" — his strategy for 401k/tax-advantaged accounts

**Retarded takes.**
- "The logic is if you don't close it, and it expires, it assumes it wax a loss" — claims this is a foolproof logic for his tracker, even though it turns expired OTM options into fake realized losses.
- "I personally max the whole 72k" — when discussing 401k limits, essentially bragging about how much he puts into tax-advantaged buckets while the rest of the room is chasing 0DTEs.
- "I be trading perps lately" — the classic 'I definitely have skin in the game' claim, despite providing zero proof.

**Recent trades.**
- Insufficient signal — too few messages on this dimension.

**Recent personal life.**
- Currently building custom Discord infrastructure to track his friends' financial failures; the room has more visibility into his coding progress than his actual life.
- Lives in Dallas for the moment, or at least he was there for a trip and complained about needing sleep to focus on his backend logic.
- Obsessed with optimizing tax-advantaged accounts like a true W2 warrior, spending late nights arguing about marginal tax rates instead of watching charts.
